Dragons Domain MUD  4.2024
Last update of this document 18/3/24



=== Preface

This document describes features available to the area builder for Dragons
Domain MUD.  We endeavour to keep it up to date to reflect stable changes in the
MUD.  It should not be used to document features that are not fully implemented
and tested.



=== Advice to builders

Plan your area on paper or using something like https://virtual-graph-paper.com/.
Check the MUD to make sure there's not already something similar in place. Map
out your area and place mobs and items BEFORE you start writing.  Think before
you write.  If you have questions, read through this document and try and figure
it out.  If you still have questions feel free to ask one of the imms. Let
your imagination run wild, but keep on theme (medieval fantasy hack and slash,
for the most part).



=== Overview of Areas:

An area is one piece of the world.  Each area is defined in a separate file.
All area files have the extension '.are'. Remember the Dragons Domain is a
magical, medieval fantasy-based world, where technology is not particularly
advanced.



=== Sections of an Area

An area file contains the following sections:

    #AREA
    #AREA_SPECIAL
    #HELPS
    #RECALL
    #MOBILES
    #MOBPROGS
    #OBJECTS
    #OBJECT_SETS
    #ROOMS
    #ROOMS_AMBIENT
    #RESETS
    #GAMES
    #SHOPS
    #SPECIALS
    #$

An area is a collection of sections starting with #AREA until the next #AREA.
All of our area files (except 'helpfile.are') contain just one #AREA section,
which is at the top of the file.


=== Data Types

All of the data in an area file (even the section headers) consists of a
series of values.  Each value has a specific type.  The server parses the
file by reading in data values one at a time according to the types it
expects.

Blank characters (spaces, tabs, new lines, carriage returns) at the beginning
of a data value are always ignored (this includes strings).  Thus, you can
format the area files whatever way suits your taste and your needs.

The individual types are: 'letter', 'word', 'string', 'number', and 'to_eol'.

A 'letter' is a single non-blank character.

A 'word' is a sequence of non-blank characters terminated by a blank.

A 'string' is a sequence of non-tilde characters terminated by a tilde.  A
tilde is this character: '~'.  Thus, strings may contain blanks, and may be
multiple lines long.  There is no limit on the length of an individual
string; however, all strings go into a common memory pool whose size is fixed
when the server is compiled.

A 'number' is a decimal number with an optional leading '-' or '+'.  The '|'
character may be used in any number: '1|64|1048576' has the value 1048641.
The individual values separated by '|' are added together, so '5|6' is 11,
not 7. The components need not be powers of 2.  This feature is extremely
useful for defining bit vectors, such as the ACT_* and AFF_* bits for
mobiles, but is not restricted to bit vectors: any number may use the '|'
construction. ie, when putting affects together, all you need to do is the
following (example is a mob who is aggressive and stays in one room) 1|2|32.

A 'to_eol' is all the characters from the current position to the end of the
current input line.  It is used for parsing comments at the ends of lines.

In the syntax description below, <value:type> indicates a value to be read of
the indicated type.  A backslash '\' indicates that the file format itself
has only one line, but several lines are used in this description to fit
within 80 columns.

Braces '{ ... }' are used to enclose elements.  They are NOT literal parts of
the file format, but a way of indicating that the enclosed elements may be
repeated zero or more times.  Braces at the same level of indentation
indicate that the parallel elements may be present in any order.

All other characters in the syntax description are literal characters.

Mobiles, objects, and rooms are identified by vnum (virtual number).  The
range of vnum's is 1 to 65534.  Vnums must be unique (for that particular
kind of vnum).  Vnums do not have to be in increasing order.

Typically an area uses the same range of vnums for mobile vnum', object
vnum', and room vnums, starting with a multiple of 100.  This facilitates
adding the area into an existing set of areas.

At DD4, we typically preassign room vnums.  Let us know the size of your area
to the nearest 100 rooms and we'll give you an available "block" of vnums to use.



=== The #AREA section

The syntax of this section is:

    #AREA <area-writer:string>~ <area-name:string>~
    <lower-level-recommended:number> <upper-level-recommended:number>  \
    <lower-level-hard-limit:number> <upper-level-hard-limit:number>

The 'area-writer' is the name of author of the area.  The 'area-name' is the
name of the area and can be any string.  The next line contains 4 numbers.  The
first two are the recommended level range of the area, and the second two are
the game-enforced limits on character levels who may enter the area.

The 'areas' command uses the first four of the these values.  An example #AREA
section follows:

    #AREA Owl~ Ota'ar Dar Prince's Canton~
    55 60 40 100



=== The #AREA_SPECIAL section

The syntax of this section is:

    #AREA_SPECIAL
    <area-flag:word>
    {
    <flag-modifier:number>
    }
    $

The 'area-flag' is a usually-standalone word put on a line by itself to indicate
a special effect and/or flag that should be applied to the entire area.
Currently one of these, 'exp_mod' also has an associated 'flag-modifier' number
that should follow it.

The current #AREA_SPECIAL flags are:

school                  Area is a MUD SCHOOL (new character beginning area).
no_quest                No mobs in this area may be auto-quest targets.
hidden                  The "areas" command will not show this area, and the
                        mapmaker will not generate maps of it.
safe                    Pkilling cannot happen in this area.
no_teleport             A player cannot teleport into this area.
no_magic                Magic cannot be used in this area.
exp_mod NUMBER          An experience point modifier is applied to all mobs
                        killed in this area. 100 means no change, 50 means
                        halved, 200 means doubled, and so forth.
reset_msg STRING        Reset message that replaces the default area-reset
                        message. Put the message on the following line and
                        terminate it with a tilde (~).
reset_sound FILE        Replaces the default notification sound played when the
                        area-reset message is shown. FILE must be a relative
                        media path containing no spaces, normally beginning with
                        'notify/'. If this setting is omitted, the default reset
                        sound is: notify/area_reset.mp3
reset_sound_vol NUMBER  Sets the source volume of the area-reset notification
                        sound. A value of 0 disables the reset sound. If this
                        setting is omitted, the default volume is 60.
ambient FILE            Default environmental ambience for the area. FILE is a
                        relative media path containing no spaces, normally
                        beginning with "ambient/".
ambient_vol NUMBER      Default area ambience volume. 0 disables the area
                        ambience; values from 1 to 100 are valid.
music FILE              Default looping music for the area. FILE is a relative
                        media path containing no spaces, normally beginning with
                        "music/".
music_vol NUMBER        Default area music volume. 0 disables the area music;
                        values from 1 to 100 are valid.

An example #AREA_SPECIAL section follows:

#AREA_SPECIAL
no_teleport
no_quest
ambient ambient/area/forest_insects.mp3
ambient_vol 30
music music/area/forest_theme.mp3
music_vol 20
reset_msg
A fell wind blows through the forest.
~
reset_sound notify/area/forest_reset.mp3
reset_sound_vol 55
$



=== The #HELPS section

Help sections are usually placed in their own file (helpfile.are) but may be
included in individual area files, too.  It will be included in the pool of all
entries accessible via the 'help' command. Conflicting helps are resolved by
displaying only the first instance of a help entry.

One quite good use of the #HELPS section in an area file is to give imms private
information about the area and how it works by restricting the help to
characters of level 101 or higher.

The syntax of a help entry is as follows:

    #HELPS
    {
	<level:number> <keywords:string>~
	<help-text:string>~
    }
    0 $~

The 'level' number is the minimum level necessary to read the help for the
given topic.  For example:

100 IMMTALK :~

would mean that only characters of level 100 or higher could read the helps
for the 'immtalk' command.

The 'keywords' are the text strings that must be matched in order to see
this particular help entry.  Case is unimportant, though typically the
keywords are capitalized.  For two-word entries, single quotes must be
placed around each phrase.  For example:

0 'CURE POISON'~

The 'help-text' of the help is completely at the discretion of the builder.
Spells usually give a syntax example, followed by a summary:

0 'CURE POISON'~
Syntax: cast 'cure poison' <victim>

This spell will remove the effects of poison from the victim's body.
~

Normally when a player uses 'help', both the keywords and the help-text are
shown.  If the 'level' is negative, however, the keywords are suppressed.  This
allows the help file mechanism to be used for certain other commands, such as
the initial 'greetings' text.

If a 'help-text' begins with a leading '.', the leading '.' is stripped off.
This provides for an escape mechanism from the usual leading-blank stripping of
strings, so that entries with complex ASCII art etc may be used.



=== The #RECALL section

The syntax of this section is:

    #RECALL <recall-point:number>

The 'recall-point' gives the vnum of the room to which a player will
recall if they attempt to do so anywhere within the area.  If this section is
omitted, the recall point will be the standard ROOM_VNUM_TEMPLE defined in
merc.h (currently vnum 3001), or your clan headquarters if your character is
clanned.  If the 'recall-point' is invalid, the player is unable to recall
within the area.



=== The #MOBILES section

Mobs are where a lot of the character of your area comes out.  This will be the
most important section for players who walk around with 'brief' on.
The syntax of this section is:

    #MOBILES
    {
     #<vnum:number>
     <keywords:string>
     <short-description:string>
     <long-description:string>
     <description:string>
     <act-flags:number> <affected-flags:number> <alignment:number> S
     <level:number> <hitroll:number> <armor:number>			\
     <hitnodice:number> d <hitsizedice:number> + <hitplus:number>	\
     <damnodice:number> d <damsizedice:number> + <damroll:number>
     <body_form:number> <exp:number>
     <position:number> <position:number> <sex:number>
     {
        {mob_foley_type_1} " " {mob_foley_key_1} "~"
            .   .   .
        {mob_foley_type_last} " " {mob_foley_key_last} "~"
     }
     {
        ">" {mobprog_trigger_type} " " {argument_list} "~"
        {program_command_1}
        {program_command_2}
        {program_command_3}
            .   .   .
        {program_command_N}
        "~" NL
        "|"
     }
     {
        < <archetype:word>~ <rank:word>~
     }
    }
    #0


The 'vnum' is the virtual number of the mobile.

The 'keywords' are words which can be used in commands to identify the
mobile.

The 'short-description' is the description used by the 'act' function and
other functions (notably in combat) to identify the mobile.

The 'long-description' is the description seen when a character walks into a
room in which the mobile is visible.

The 'description' is the longest description.  It is used when a character
explicitly looks at the mobile.

The 'act-flags' define how the mobile acts.  See 'HELP BITZ' in game to see what
numbers the BIT_ values resolve to.

ACT_ flags:
    ACT_IS_NPC              BIT_0      Auto set for mobs
    ACT_SENTINEL            BIT_1      Stays in one room
    ACT_SCAVENGER           BIT_2      Picks up objects
    ACT_QUESTMASTER         BIT_3      Runs automated quests
    ACT_AGGRESSIVE          BIT_5      Attacks PC's
    ACT_STAY_AREA           BIT_6      Won't leave area
    ACT_WIMPY               BIT_7      Flees when hurt
    ACT_PET                 BIT_8      Auto set for pets
    ACT_NO_QUEST            BIT_9      Cannot be selected as quest target
    ACT_PRACTICE            BIT_10     Can teach PCs skills/spells
    ACT_REGENERATOR         BIT_11     Heals at an accelerated rate
    ACT_NOCHARM             BIT_12     Not charmable
    ACT_IS_HEALER           BIT_13     Can use the healer special
    ACT_IS_FAMOUS           BIT_14     Award fame for killing
    ACT_LOSE_FAME           BIT_15     Lose fame for killing
    ACT_WIZINVIS_MOB        BIT_16     Can't be seen by players
    ACT_MOUNTABLE           BIT_17     Can be ridden
    ACT_TINKER              BIT_18     Can repair equipment
    ACT_BANKER              BIT_19     Is a banker/vault master
    ACT_IDENTIFY            BIT_20     Mob can identify objects
    ACT_DIE_IF_MASTER_GONE  BIT_21     Is destroyed if its master is not in room
    ACT_CLAN_GUARD          BIT_22     A clan guard, has some combat resistances
    ACT_NO_SUMMON           BIT_23     Mob may not be summoned
    ACT_NO_EXPERIENCE       BIT_24     No exp rewarded for killing this mob
    ACT_NO_HEAL             BIT_25     Will not heal HP from damage, supercedes
                                       ACT_REGENERATOR
    ACT_NO_FIGHT            BIT_26     No active attacks in fights (passive
                                       damaging like fireshield still works)
    ACT_OBJECT              BIT_27     Acts as an attackable object
    ACT_INVULNERABLE        BIT_28     Cannot be physically damaged
    ACT_BLINK               BIT_29     Mob access to the "blink" ability
    ACT_UNDEAD              BIT_30     Mob is undead. Various implications.
    ACT_FEAR_AURA           BIT_31     Visible combat opponents face one fear
                                       save per fight
    ACT_UNKILLABLE          BIT_63     Cannot be killed. 'slay' still works

The 'affected-flags' define more attributes of the mobile.

AFF_ flags:
    AFF_BLIND               BIT_0      Can't see
    AFF_INVISIBLE           BIT_1      Can't be seen without detect invis
    AFF_DETECT_EVIL         BIT_2      Can detect evil
    AFF_DETECT_INVIS        BIT_3      Can see invisible things
    AFF_DETECT_MAGIC        BIT_4      Can detect magic
    AFF_DETECT_HIDDEN       BIT_5      Can detect hidden players/mobs
    AFF_HOLD                BIT_6      Is held/trapped
    AFF_SANCTUARY           BIT_7      Is affected by the sanctuary spell
    AFF_FAERIE_FIRE         BIT_8      Is affected by faerie fire
    AFF_INFRARED            BIT_9      Can see in the dark
    AFF_CURSE               BIT_10     Is cursed
    AFF_FLAMING             BIT_11     Has fireshield on
    AFF_POISON              BIT_12     Is poisoned
    AFF_PROTECT             BIT_13     Is affected by the protection spell
    AFF_MEDITATE            BIT_14     Is meditating
    AFF_SNEAK               BIT_15     Is sneaking
    AFF_HIDE                BIT_16     Is hiding
    AFF_SLEEP               BIT_17     Is asleep
    AFF_CHARM               BIT_18     Has been charmed
    AFF_FLYING              BIT_19     Is flying
    AFF_PASS_DOOR           BIT_20     Can pass through non-passproof doors
    AFF_DETECT_TRAPS        BIT_21     Can detect trapped items
    AFF_BATTLE_AURA         BIT_22     Affected by battle aura (dam reduction)
    AFF_DETECT_SNEAK        BIT_23     Can detect sneaking PCs/NPCs
    AFF_GLOBE               BIT_24     Has globe spell on
    AFF_DETER               BIT_25     Deters hostile creatures
    AFF_SWIM                BIT_26     Is swimming
    AFF_PRAYER_PLAGUE       BIT_27     Is very sick indeed
    AFF_NON_CORPOREAL       BIT_28     Doesn't interact with the world much
    AFF_DETECT_CURSE        BIT_29     Will attack the cursed
    AFF_DETECT_GOOD         BIT_30     Can detect good-aligned
    AFF_SWALLOWED           BIT_31     Has been swallowed by a large creature
    AFF_NO_RECALL           BIT_32     Cannot recall
    AFF_DOT                 BIT_33     Takes damage over time
    AFF_PRONE               BIT_34     Can't do skills, can cast
    AFF_DAZED               BIT_35     Can't do anything
    AFF_CONFUSION           BIT_36     Wanders
    AFF_MINDLESS            BIT_49     Lacks a mind. See "Mindlessness" below.
    AFF_STENCH              BIT_50     Emits carrion stench.
    AFF_SLOW                BIT_63     Slowed down. Many negative consequences.

==Mindlessness

AFF_MINDLESS uses BIT_49, decimal 562949953421312.
Scribe supplies it through the existing affect field:

    aff mindless

This is an explicit trait, independent of undead classification, anatomy,
intelligence scores and physiology. No existing archetype is automatically
assigned it by this chunk.

The ordinary AFF XOR rules apply. An individual bit adds mindlessness when
it is absent from the template, or cancels it when already inherited.
Omission or zero preserves an inherited bit.

Implemented mind-dependent consumers:

    Synaptic Blast, Inflict Pain, Mind Thrust, Psionic Blast,
    Psychic Crush, and each otherwise eligible Ultrablast target.

    Charm Person, Awe, Ego Whip, Feeblemind, Psychic Drain,
    Fear, Confusion, and Possession.

    Combat Mind, which requires a mind to enhance.

    Transfix, and the defensive Aura of Fear check against its attacker.

The target checks occur before the effect is applied. They do not roll a
saving throw or modify the resistance masks.

Mindlessness does not automatically grant RES_PSYCHIC immunity. Physical
psionic effects such as Ballistic Attack and Project Force retain their
existing handling. The list above defines this chunk's implemented consumers;
it is not a blanket rule for all psionic or psychologically named abilities.

Existing casting costs, lag, target restrictions and surrounding retaliation
remain in place. Rejecting the effect is not a ceasefire or a mana refund.
Domination's existing disabled implementation is not re-enabled.

mstat's existing AFF layers display the named flag. Live editing uses:

    mset <mobile> mindless on
    mset <mobile> mindless off

These NPC-only commands set or clear only this live bit. They do not change
the prototype, other flags, other instances or future spawns. Repeating the
same setting is idempotent. Invalid settings change nothing.

Adding the trait does not retroactively remove existing effects or follower
relationships. No generic rejection is added to affect_to_char() or
add_follower(): necromantic servants may still use the existing AFF_CHARM
and follower machinery.

Mindlessness does not itself grant poison, sleep, paralysis or drain immunity,
remove body parts, change INT/WIS, disable special functions or prevent speech.
The skeleton's physiology and resistance package remain separate work.


The 'alignment' of the mobile ranges from -1000 to +1000.  Keep in mind that
certain spells give characters fighting oppositely-aligned monsters an
advantage, and that experience gained is influenced by the relative alignments
of a character and their victims.

The literal letter 'S' must be present after the alignment.  In the original
Diku mob format, 'S' stands for simple.  DD4 supports only 'simple' mobs, so
the 'S' is actually redundant, but is retained for compatibility.

The 'level' is a number from 1 to (probably?) 2147483647.  Mobs power scale up
to INT_MAX, but it's unlikely you'd make mobs much higher level than 120 or so,
given that a player maxes at 100--assuming you actually want them to be killed
by players (which you may not).

The 'body-form' is a value that describes the morphology of mobiles, to improve
corpse and carnage production upon death, and prevent some rather silly
player/mobile interactions (tripping up snakes, for example). It contains flags
that describe the physical characteristics of mobiles that are checked by
many functions during the game.

A body_form value of 0 specifies a normal-sized humanoid capable of speech.

The following bit values are used to specify mob form:

        Flag              Bit   Description
        --------------    ---   --------------------------------
        BODY_NO_HEAD        1   No head
        BODY_NO_EYES        2   No eyes
        BODY_NO_ARMS        4   No arms
        BODY_NO_LEGS        8   No legs
        BODY_NO_HEART      16   No heart
        BODY_NO_SPEECH     32   Cannot speak the common language
        BODY_NO_CORPSE     64   Won't leave a corpse when killed
        BODY_HUGE         128   Massive size
        BODY_INORGANIC    256   Not made of living tissue
        BODY_HAS_TAIL     512   Has a tail
        BODY_BLOODLESS    BIT_46  No usable blood
        BODY_ANALGIC      BIT_47  Insensible to pain
        BODY_SOULLESS     BIT_48  No soul to torment or harvest


Blood, pain and soul properties
------------------------------


Holy symbols and priest services
-------------------------------

ACT_PRIEST_SERVICES is BIT_32 (4294967296), and ACT_UNDEAD_HEALER is
BIT_33 (8589934592). In Scribe, use act healer priest_services or
act healer undead_healing. Both capabilities can be given to one mobile.
Neither capability supplies the base healer flag. Existing mobiles keep
their ordinary healer menu until a builder adds a capability.

Priest services add:
    heal restoration            Clear Drained without refilling resources, 40 gold
    heal detect                 Detect Undead, 5 gold
    heal protection             Protection vs Evil, 10 gold
    heal ward                   Protection vs Undead, 20 gold
    heal blessweapon <weapon>   100 + 5 * the weapon's current level, in gold
    heal refill <container>     8 gold per 15 missing liquid units, rounded up

Restoration is refused before payment when there is no Drained condition.
It does not restore current HP, mana or movement. Vitalize remains the
ordinary 150-gold service that clears Drained and fills those resources.

Bless Weapon uses the existing permanent spell and all its item checks.
The weapon must be carried, unworn, unblessed and free of existing affects.
It also receives the spell's normal mana and saving-throw affects. Casting
level is the lower of customer and priest level, bounded to normal levels.
The price uses the weapon level before the spell changes it.

Refills accept an empty clean drink container, or a partially full one
holding clean holy water. They fill its remaining capacity. Mixed or
poisoned liquid is refused. A full container is refused before payment.
A 15-unit refill costs 8 gold; 30 units cost 16 gold.

Undead healing adds heal mend (100 HP, 15 gold) and heal greatermend
(300 HP, 35 gold). Vampire players receive the full restoration. These
services do not restore blood, mana, aggravated damage or lost levels.
The normal HP ceiling and ACT_NO_HEAL still apply. Living customers and
undead with no healable HP missing are refused before payment. Neither
service changes alignment.

Midgaard's healer (mobile 3012, room 3054) now offers priest services and
sells wooden holy symbols (object 3395) and holy-water vials (object 3396).
His shop is open all day. Base list prices are 25 and 10 gold; normal
shop discounts and customer restrictions still apply. A full vial holds
15 liquid units, enough for three normal splashes. Saul (mobile 9540,
room 9547 in the Vampire Catacombs) offers undead mending. These changes
do not convert either mobile to a new species or change any room flags.

ITEM_HOLY_FOCUS is BIT_42 (4398046511104), named ex holy_focus in Scribe.
A living creature gets a bonus only from a focus equipped at either neck
slot or in the hold slot. A cursed focus contributes nothing. Carrying a
spare does nothing, and multiple equipped foci use only the best bonus.
Undead and noncorporeal players receive no focus bonus; carrying a symbol
does not burn them.

A neck focus adds 5 points to the turning base; a held focus adds 10.
The learned skill percentage scales that base as before. At full skill,
ordinary equal-level undead go from 50% to 55% worn or 60% held. The bonus
works with both Turn Undead and Chant of Turning. Existing chance caps,
level limits, PvP rules and destruction chances are unchanged.

Either position also adds 5 points to an existing spell saving throw
against an undead source. It stacks with existing spell protections,
then uses the normal save limits. It adds no new saving throw, immunity,
general combat stats or physical damage reduction. ITEM_BLESS alone does
not make a weapon or another object a holy focus.

The normal 64-bit object and mobile flag fields carry these properties.
Identify and Lore describe holy_focus; OSTAT and MSTAT name the new flags.
The automatic adept, orb, hooker, Grail and bounty support routines and
paid healer services share the harmful-help check. They skip hostile
Bless on undead while keeping vampire holy healing at 75% and Regenerate
normal. Sleeping targets can still receive helpful spells. Intentional
offensive casting is unchanged. No new learnable skill or spell is added.

Additional body_form bits:

    BODY_BLOODLESS  BIT_46   70368744177664
    BODY_ANALGIC    BIT_47   140737488355328
    BODY_SOULLESS   BIT_48   281474976710656

Scribe names these bf bloodless, bf analgic and bf soulless.
ANALGIC means insensible to pain. These are restrictions, not positive
body parts, resistance categories or natural attack parts.

A set flag forces its restriction. An absent flag still allows the existing
tests to reject the target. Inorganic bodies and object mobiles cannot
supply blood; mindless targets still resist Inflict Pain; ordinary object
mobiles cannot feel pain or supply a soul. Existing resistance, saving,
form and combat checks keep applying. Do not infer soullessness or ANALGIC
from undead or inorganic status alone.

Skeleton and zombie templates add all three body flags. Individual masks
still use XOR: repeating a supplied flag removes it. Removing one flag does
not remove other causes of ineligibility. For example, an inorganic
skeleton stays bloodless after cancelling BODY_BLOODLESS; a mindless zombie
still rejects Inflict Pain after cancelling BODY_ANALGIC.

The new corpse/remains markers are ITEM_BLOODLESS (BIT_40) and ITEM_SOULLESS
(BIT_41). Creation records the effective answer, including inferred traits.
Normal 64-bit ExtraFlags save/load preserves them. FEED rejects bloodless
or undead NPC corpses; STEAL SOUL rejects soulless NPC corpses. Player
corpses and loose body parts remain unsuitable. Neither marker affects
an unrelated object's wearer or carrier.

Use mstat's effective blood/pain/soul row alongside its raw flags.
These additions do not add a new area record or convert existing mobiles
to species types. That content migration remains a later task.


Example:
        #101
        wild dog^
        a wild dog^
        A half starved, wild dog wanders the abandoned street.
        ~
        This wild-eyed and starving mongrel patrols the piles of rubbish.
        ~
        1|32|128 0 0 S
        1 0 0 0d0+0 0d0+0
Here -> 4|32 0
        8 8 1

In this example, the mob 'wild dog' has a body_form value of 36 (no arms
and no speech).  Pipes can be used for easy calculation.

Mobs with 'BODY_NO_CORPSE' set do not form corpses or produce carnage; they
wither and disappear. Any coins or equipment that would normally be in a corpse
falls to the floor of the room.  If equipment or money falls a message is printed
when the mob is killed.

Corpse forming mobiles may produce body parts when killed or spray blood.
Random death-generated parts use the creature's resolved BODY/PART anatomy
and declared natural parts. Legacy default head, heart and limb checks remain.
A successful part-drop roll creates at most one eligible kind. Quantity flags
do not create several objects, and BODY_NO_CORPSE suppresses all such drops.

Generated parts decay, carry ITEM_BODY_PART, and retain undead provenance.
Inorganic parts are inedible trash. See the Scribe manual for supported
part keywords and examples. mstat lists the current death-part candidates.

Body parts from mobiles with 'BODY_INORGANIC' set are made type
'trash' and are inedible (but still have a timer set to prevent clutter).

The "mob_foley_type" and  "mob_foley_key" values are what allow you to
optionally override default mobile foley sounds.  Currently footstep foley is
implemented by terrain type.  The default footstep sounds are those of a
medium-sized humanoid moving through SECT_CITY, SECT_SWAMP, etc.  To override
them you can use types and keys per below, where "3060" in the list below is the
mob's vnum.  Any sounds not overridden will use the defaults.

    FStepInside sfx.foley.mob.3060.inside~
    FStepCity sfx.foley.mob.3060.city~
    FStepField sfx.foley.mob.3060.field~
    FStepForest sfx.foley.mob.3060.forest~
    FStepHills sfx.foley.mob.3060.hills~
    FStepMountain sfx.foley.mob.3060.mountain~
    FStepDesert sfx.foley.mob.3060.desert~
    FStepSwamp sfx.foley.mob.3060.swamp~
    FStepWater sfx.foley.mob.3060.water~
    FStepUnderwater sfx.foley.mob.3060.underwater~
    FStepUnderwaterGround sfx.foley.mob.3060.underwater_ground~
    FStepAir sfx.foley.mob.3060.air~

You will need to add entries to the sound_const.c file for these overrides, and
upload the sounds in the appropriate server directories for Mudlet to pull them,
e.g.

{ "sfx.foley.mob.3060.city",
    { "sfx/foley/mob/3060/city.1.mp3",
      "sfx/foley/mob/3060/city.2.mp3", NULL }, { 0 }, 45, "foley", 1 },

{ "sfx.foley.mob.3060.inside",
    { "sfx/foley/mob/3060/inside.1.mp3",
      "sfx/foley/mob/3060/inside.2.mp3", NULL }, { 0 }, 45, "foley", 1 },

The "mobprog_trigger_type" and associated information describe the format of
mobprogs, simple mobile-specific programs written in the MUD's pseudo-scripting
Mobprog language.  Their description is out of scope of this document, but read
mobprogs.txt for full details.

The optional mobile selection line names an archetype and rank:

    < skeleton~ common~

Scribe supplies these independently through archetype and rnk. The selected
archetype determines the body species and inherited defaults.

ACT, AFF, body-form, attack-part and resistance inputs are individual XOR
masks. Scalar values replace or inherit defaults. Special names resolve per
slot, with one complete selection-probability vector.

Export the original individual inputs, not an expanded inherited state.
Use mstat to inspect template, individual, prototype and live values.

Consult mob.c or the Scribe manual for individual archetypes.

Dwarf archetype and creature families
------------------------------------

    < dwarf~ common~

The dwarf inherits a normal living humanoid body, AFF_INFRARED and
RES_MAGIC | RES_POISON. Its internal traits grant goblinoid training and
giant defence without consuming ACT bits.
It has no default active special, immunity or vulnerability. Its HP, damage,
critical and swiftness modifiers are zero; its XP adjustment is +10 points.
The descriptive height/weight defaults are 130/70, using centimetres and
kilograms for these converted dwarf entries. Raw metadata does not control
gameplay size; size and language codes remain unspecified.

Training uses ordinary melee rolls: +1 hitroll against orcs, half-orcs,
goblins and hobgoblins; ogres, trolls, ogre magi, giants and titans take -4
hitroll against a defender with giant defence. Shoot and Snap Shot are
excluded. Neither actor's stored hitroll or AC is changed.

Use the existing archetype selection for a dwarf without either training
benefit:

    < dwarf_untrained~ common~

This variant shares all ordinary dwarf defaults and dwarf family identity.
Its internal trait profile disables both trained benefits. There are no new
area fields or individual trait overrides. ACT bits 34-36 are retired and
reserved. Ordinary flag masks retain their existing XOR rules.

Creature family is mob_type.family; the optional length scalar follows it. Old templates
retain NONE until reviewed. Minimal humanoid target entries remain for
half_orc, hobgoblin, ogre, ogre_mage, giant and titan.
Their full traits are unfinished; they supply identity and anatomy only.
Goblin, human and orc now have the reviewed defaults described below.
The new elf profile has its approved defences, with further traits deferred.
Troll now has the reviewed physiology and abilities in Troll Den creatures.
No existing mobs are automatically converted by their names.

Player Dwarves receive the same melee training benefits. Player Orcs,
Goblins, Hobgoblins, Ogres, Trolls and Giants count as those opponent
families. This does not import NPC resistance packages into player races.
MSTAT shows the effective family and both training abilities.

Dwarven Homestead 20500-20519 is the first area conversion. Its child and
teen use dwarf_untrained; its caretaker and servant use regeneration. The
servant's unused practice marker becomes NO_QUEST, the thief loses the
accidental tinker flag, and guests/maids lose pass-door. Equipment, teacher
declarations, games and room flags retain their existing definitions.

Goblin and human archetypes
---------------------------

    < goblin~ common~
    < human~ common~

Both inherit the normal living, speaking humanoid body. Goblins add
PART_FANGS as anatomy, AFF_INFRARED and the internal sunlight_sensitive trait. The empty
natural attack mask does not supply an automatic bite. Goblin defaults are
height 120, weight 30 and CHAR_SIZE_SMALL metadata. Human defaults are
175, 75 and CHAR_SIZE_MEDIUM. Height/weight use the descriptive cm/kg
convention; these fields do not change equipment fitting or combat size.
Language remains unspecified. Neither supplies a default active special,
resistance, vulnerability or immunity. All combat and XP modifiers are zero.

The sunlight_sensitive trait gives an NPC -1 effective attack hitroll in
clear daytime outside:
SUN_LIGHT and SKY_CLOUDLESS, outside an indoors/inside, underwater, DARK or
NO_WEATHER room. It applies to ordinary melee and Shoot. Snap Shot remains
player-only and is unaffected by this NPC trait. The trait does
not change stored hitroll, cause damage or restrict movement. The trait is
available to any NPC; player ACT bits never grant it.

A different sunlight policy needs an appropriate archetype defined in code.
Traits belong only to species and archetypes; no area field adds, cancels or
adjusts one. Infravision and anatomy inherit independently. MSTAT shows the
effective trait, signed strength, source and current hitroll adjustment.

Passive trait definitions and sparse resolved lists live in mob.c. Trait IDs
are registry indexes, not flag positions. Species defaults precede archetype
changes; archetypes may share a trait parent within the same species. An
omitted trait inherits, a supplied strength replaces it, and zero disables
it. A repeated trait does not stack with itself. Different trait IDs can
contribute to the same roll when their rules match. Target-family groups
are lists, so neither traits nor target groups have a 64-bit ceiling.
Startup validates profiles and inheritance. Current strengths are -100 to
100. Active special functions keep their existing slots and selection.

Goblin Caves 20002-20016 now use goblin, while 20000, 20001 and 20017 use
human. The wizard's existing role gains spec_cast_mage through SPECIALS;
it is not part of the human archetype. Keep individual protection and all
other existing encounter flags and equipment separate from racial defaults.
No matching Scribe source exists for this area.

Player Goblin abilities and sunlight rules are unchanged. The human family
also recognises the Human player race without adding a racial ability.
The goblin's disease/construction traits are deferred for later review.

Dwarven Kingdom and reptile bodies
---------------------------------

The new quadrupedal_reptile and serpentine_reptile body species provide
ordinary living reptile anatomy. Both have a head, tail, scales, heart,
brain and guts, with no speech or humanoid arms. The first has four legs
and feet; the second has no legs. They supply neutral modifiers and no
natural attack, special or resistance by themselves.

    < giant_lizard~ common~
    < giant_pit_viper~ common~

Both archetypes add anatomical fangs and one natural bite damage type.
Giant lizard has descriptive length 457 cm and large size metadata, with
no default active special. Giant pit viper has length 366 cm and medium
size metadata, infrared heat sight and spec_poison. All combat/XP modifiers
are zero. Neither gains default resistance or immunity. The lizard does not
gain additional claw/tail attacks, and jaw-grip behaviour awaits review.

MobLength is an optional absolute scalar in centimetres. It uses the same
inherit/explicit-zero and validation rules as MobHeight, and defaults to
unspecified on older templates. It follows family at the end of mob_type
and initial_ghost_phase at the end of species_type/mob_template_data.
The loader copies resolved length to the live NPC. MSTAT shows every layer,
and MSET length changes live NPC metadata. It has no physical gameplay effect.

Dwarven Kingdom 6500-6517 now selects the agreed dwarf, human, wraith,
giant and reptile entries. Twelve legacy ACT_REGENERATOR bits are removed.
The existing wraith keeps an individual no-speech mask but inherits its
no-corpse bit once. Its senses include infrared. The doctor's adept special
is individual. The giant has MobHeight 244, MobSize 2 and MobDamMod 10.
The snake is now a neutral giant pit viper with a real heart. Shop, object,
room, exit and reset data keep their existing definitions. There is no
matching tracked Scribe source for this area.

The wight archetype is an intelligent corporeal undead humanoid:

    < wight~ common~

It inherits ordinary humanoid anatomy and adds ACT_UNDEAD.

Wights are not mindless and retain speech. The archetype does not add
BODY_INORGANIC, BODY_NO_CORPSE, BODY_NO_SPEECH or ghost phase.

Passive combat defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_DARK
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_DRAIN

Wights do not inherit RES_NONMAGIC, silver vulnerability, cold-iron
vulnerability, charm immunity or paralysis immunity.

The template supplies spec_wight as its active behaviour. It does not use
the broad legacy spec_cast_undead spellcaster.

While fighting and able to act, spec_wight has a 20 percent chance at each
special-function opportunity to attempt Energy Drain against the current
opponent.

The shared Energy Drain implementation remains authoritative. It uses
RES_DRAIN | RES_MAGIC, its normal save, a small damaging hit and Drained.
The target's 30-second protection limits new stacks, not ordinary damage.
spec_wight does not use this attack against an IS_UNDEAD() opponent.

The archetype XP adjustment remains zero. spec_wight receives the normal
+10 percentage-point special-function XP weighting.

A normally killed wight retains the ordinary corporeal death path. Its
corpse is marked by the existing undead-provenance machinery.

Scribe selects the archetype with "archetype wight". Individual ACT,
resistance, vulnerability and immunity values remain XOR overrides.

Do not convert existing world wights merely by name. Conversion should be
performed explicitly after the archetype and spec_wight have been tested.

The wraith archetype is an intelligent supernatural undead spirit:

    < wraith~ common~

It uses the humanoid body species but has no ordinary biological interior.

Its body-form XOR adds:

    BODY_NO_HEART
    BODY_NO_CORPSE
    BODY_INORGANIC

and cancels inherited:

    PART_HEART
    PART_BRAINS
    PART_GUTS

Humanoid limbs and sensory capability remain inherited. The wraith is not
mindless and retains speech.

Passive combat defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_DARK
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_DRAIN | RES_NONMAGIC

The wraith starts with ghostphase semi_material.

RES_NONMAGIC supplies the mundane-contact barrier. The existing supported
phase-aware weapon/shield rules therefore apply: ordinary mundane weapons are
blocked, mundane silver receives the established semi-material exception, and
magical weapons use normal resistance handling.

The template does not assign AFF_NON_CORPOREAL. Live NPC phase remains the
separate ghost_phase state.

The wraith archetype supplies spec_wraith.

When not fighting and otherwise able to act, a semi-material wraith changes
to ghostphase ethereal on its next special-function opportunity.

When an ethereal wraith enters combat, its next eligible special-function
opportunity changes it to semi_material. That materialisation uses the
opportunity; it does not also attempt life drain.

While fighting in semi_material, spec_wraith reuses the existing spec_wight
Energy Drain behaviour, including its 20 percent attempt chance,
RES_DRAIN | RES_MAGIC handling, save behaviour and shared Drained stacks.

An explicit ghostphase none disables the automatic phase transitions without
disabling life drain or removing RES_NONMAGIC.

After combat ends, a semi-material wraith returns to ethereal at its next
eligible special opportunity.

Charm, hold, daze, wait state, unconsciousness and death suppress both phase
transitions and drain attempts.

A fully ethereal wraith cannot use or be reached by the currently supported
physical weapon/shield contacts with an unphased character, even with a
magical weapon. Semi-material contact uses the established mundane/silver/
magical rules.

These live phases do not themselves alter visibility, movement, unrelated
spells or AFF_NON_CORPOREAL.

Wraiths leave no biological corpse. Normal no-corpse equipment and coin
handling remains in use.

spec_wraith receives the normal +10 percentage-point special-function XP
weighting. The archetype's own XP adjustment remains zero.

Do not assign the broad spec_cast_undead special merely because the creature
is undead.

Scribe selects it with "archetype wraith". Individual flag fields remain XOR
overrides and ghostphase remains an independently inherited scalar.

Do not convert existing world wraiths merely by name.

The spectre archetype is an intelligent supernatural undead spirit:

    < spectre~ common~

Its passive physiology follows the established wraith spirit profile.

The body-form XOR adds:

    BODY_NO_HEART
    BODY_NO_CORPSE
    BODY_INORGANIC

and cancels inherited:

    PART_HEART
    PART_BRAINS
    PART_GUTS

The spectre retains humanoid limbs and sensory capability. It is intelligent
and retains speech.

Passive combat defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_DARK
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_DRAIN | RES_NONMAGIC
                    | RES_COLD | RES_CHARM | RES_HOLD | RES_PARALYSIS
    dimensions:     height 175 cm, Medium; mass and length unspecified
    internal trait: sunlight_powerless

The initial ghostphase is semi_material.

The template supplies spec_spectre. It uses the same automatic phase model as
spec_wraith: while not fighting, an eligible semi-material spectre becomes
ethereal; when an ethereal spectre enters combat, its next eligible special
opportunity changes it to semi_material and consumes that opportunity.

While fighting in semi_material, or when explicitly configured with
ghostphase none, spec_spectre has a 30 percent chance at each eligible special
opportunity to attempt Energy Drain. Wraiths retain their existing 20 percent
chance.

The Energy Drain implementation itself remains shared and authoritative.
RES_DRAIN | RES_MAGIC resistance, the normal save, damage and shared
Drained condition apply. The shared 30-second protection limits stacks
while ordinary damage can continue. IS_UNDEAD() targets are left alone.

An explicit ghostphase none disables automatic spectre phase transitions
without disabling life drain or removing RES_NONMAGIC.

Charm, hold, daze, wait state, unconsciousness and death suppress the
special's voluntary phase transitions and drain attempts. Direct natural
sunlight can still expose a held, charmed or waiting ethereal spirit.
Sunlight stops ordinary attacks, life drain and automatic ethereal hiding;
an explicit ghostphase none stays none. It causes no burn and does not
prevent movement or fleeing. See Walls of Anon creatures for exposure rules.

A fully ethereal spectre cannot use or be reached by the currently supported
physical weapon/shield contacts with an unphased character, even with a
magical weapon. Semi-material contact uses the established mundane, silver
and magical rules.

These live phases do not themselves alter visibility, room movement,
AFF_NON_CORPOREAL or unrelated spell/effect paths.

Spectres leave no biological corpse. Existing no-corpse equipment and coin
handling remains in use.

spec_spectre contributes +15 percentage points through the special-function
XP weighting. The archetype's own XP adjustment remains zero.

The broad spec_cast_undead special is not inherited. The existing
spec_spectral_minion is a Bastion-specific teleporting special and is not
spectre archetype behaviour.

Scribe selects the archetype with "archetype spectre". Individual flag fields
remain XOR overrides and ghostphase remains an independently inherited scalar.

Do not convert existing world spectres merely by name.

The mummy archetype is an intelligent corporeal undead humanoid:

    < mummy~ common~

It inherits ordinary humanoid anatomy and ACT_UNDEAD. It is not mindless,
retains speech, remains corporeal and uses ghostphase none.

Passive combat defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_DARK | RES_BLUNT | RES_PIERCE | RES_SLASH
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY | RES_FIRE
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_CHARM | RES_DRAIN
                    | RES_COLD | RES_HOLD | RES_PARALYSIS

Mummies do not inherit RES_NONMAGIC, silver/cold-iron interaction or spirit
phase behaviour. Physical resistance halves qualifying ordinary weapon
damage. Natural armour is -7. Defaults are 175 cm/45 kg/Medium, Bloodless
and Analgic, retaining organs, consciousness, soul and physical remains.

The template supplies spec_mummy. It does not use the broad legacy
spec_cast_undead spellcaster.

While fighting, awake and otherwise able to act, spec_mummy has a 20 percent
chance on each selected special-function opportunity to attempt the existing
Fear spell against its current opponent.

Mindless and object-like targets are ineligible. The existing Fear
implementation remains authoritative for RES_MAGIC | RES_PSYCHIC and its
normal saving-throw behaviour.

Mummy rot is a separate physical-contact disease. It is not the existing
fleshrot spell.

Only a successful damaging ordinary NPC attack made without a wielded weapon
can transmit mummy rot. Weapon attacks cannot transmit it. The shared
natural-contact stage now includes all weaponless NPC ordinary attacks;
existing ghoul and ghast paralysis remains restricted to claw and bite
attacks by its own helper.

The live spec_mummy weight controls whether the contact capability is active.
With the archetype's default 100-percent singleton special, each qualifying
damaging contact has a 25 percent chance to attempt mummy rot. Lower
builder-configured spec_mummy weights reduce contact opportunities
proportionally.

Objects, inorganic creatures and victims already carrying mummy rot are not
eligible.

Player Resist Toxin and Bonus Exotic defenses apply. Otherwise the contact
uses:

    RES_POISON | RES_CURSE | RES_DARK

RES_MAGIC is deliberately absent because mummy rot is transmitted by physical
contact rather than cast as a spell.

A failed save applies mummy rot for 6-10 normal affect ticks. The effect gives:

    Constitution: -2

Once per character update while the effect remains, it also deals damage from:

    max(2, victim level / 10)

to:

    max(low value, victim level / 5)

Mummy rot does not stack or refresh while already present.

Cure Poison removes mummy rot. Its eligibility checks also now correctly
recognise standalone fleshrot; previously its later fleshrot-removal code was
unreachable when fleshrot was the victim's only relevant condition.

spec_mummy receives +15 percentage points through the special-function XP
weighting. The mummy archetype's own XP adjustment remains zero.

Disabling spec_mummy removes both new Fear attempts and new mummy-rot contact
attempts. It does not remove mummy rot already affecting a victim.

A normally killed mummy retains the ordinary corporeal death path. Its corpse
is marked by the existing undead-provenance machinery.

Scribe selects it with "archetype mummy". Individual ACT, AFF, body,
resistance, vulnerability, immunity, special and special-weight inputs retain
their normal override behaviour.

Do not convert existing world mummies merely by name.

The banshee archetype is an intelligent undead spirit:

    < banshee~ common~

It starts semi-material and has these defaults:

    resistance:     RES_DARK
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_DRAIN | RES_NONMAGIC

A banshee is not mindless and can speak. Its body is inorganic, has no heart
and leaves no corpse. The inherited humanoid heart, brains and guts are
removed.

Ordinary nonmagical weapons cannot hurt a banshee. Silver and magical weapons
use the normal semi-material ghost rules. Banshees do not automatically change
between ethereal and semi-material form.

The template supplies spec_banshee.

During combat, spec_banshee has a 20 percent chance on each special check to
wail at its current opponent. Objects and mindless creatures are unaffected.

The target saves against:

    RES_SONIC | RES_PSYCHIC

On a failed save it makes the same forced flee used by Fear. The wail does no
direct damage. It cannot be used in a cone_of_silence room.

spec_banshee adds +15 percent to the special-function XP calculation. The
archetype itself adds no extra XP.

Banshees leave no corpse; equipment and coins use the normal no-corpse death
handling.

Scribe selects the archetype with "archetype banshee". Individual mobile
fields can override the inherited defaults normally.

Existing world banshees are not changed automatically.

The death_knight archetype is an intelligent corporeal undead warrior:

    < death_knight~ common~

Its defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_DARK
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_DRAIN

Death knights are not mindless and use normal humanoid anatomy. They are
corporeal, so ordinary weapons can hurt them and they do not use ghost phases.

The template supplies spec_death_knight.

In combat, the special mixes martial attacks and spells. About half its actions
are physical: a shield smash where possible, a kick, or an extra normal attack.

Its spells are curse, chill touch and harm. Lower-level death knights use weaker
spells until they are high enough level for the stronger ones.

In a cone_of_silence room, a spell action becomes an extra normal attack
instead.

spec_death_knight adds +15 percent to the special-function XP calculation. The
archetype itself adds no extra XP.

Death knights leave a normal corpse when killed. The corpse is marked as
coming from an undead creature.

Scribe selects the archetype with "archetype death_knight". Individual mobile
fields can override the inherited defaults normally.

Existing world death knights are not changed automatically.

The vampire archetype is an intelligent corporeal undead humanoid:

    < vampire~ common~

Vampires keep normal humanoid anatomy, including their heart, brains and guts.
They are not mindless or inorganic, and they regenerate much faster than
ordinary mobiles.

Their defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_COLD | RES_ELECTRICITY | RES_DARK | RES_PSYCHIC
    vulnerability:  RES_SILVER | RES_HOLY | RES_WATER
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_CHARM | RES_CURSE
                    | RES_NONMAGIC

Hold and paralysis are not resisted by the archetype.

Ordinary nonmagical weapons cannot hurt a vampire, but a material the vampire
is vulnerable to can get through that immunity. A mundane silver weapon can
therefore hurt a vampire and receives the normal silver vulnerability bonus.

Holy attacks are especially effective against vampires. Water-based attacks
use the vampire's water vulnerability.

A blessed weapon gets the normal anti-undead bonus, but blessing alone does not
make a mundane weapon magical.

The template supplies spec_vampire.

In combat, half of its special choices make a blood-draining bite using the
normal suck attack. This is a normal combat hit; it does not add aggro damage.
If the target is a Vampire player, they lose 10 percent of their current blood
points only when the bite actually lowers their hit points.

One quarter of its special choices use a hypnotic gaze. Objects, mindless
creatures, blind targets and creatures without eyes cannot be caught by it.
The target resists with:

    RES_CHARM | RES_PSYCHIC

A failed save costs the victim two combat pulses of action.

The remaining quarter produces no special action.

The old fireball and acid-blast choices are no longer part of spec_vampire.

spec_mast_vampire uses the same suck attack for its bite, while retaining its
stronger fear and prismatic-spray behaviour.

spec_vampire receives the normal +10 percent special-function XP contribution.
The vampire archetype itself adds no extra XP.

Vampires feed on the life force carried in living blood. FEED refuses a corpse
marked ITEM_UNDEAD because any blood remaining in it no longer contains the
living essence needed to nourish a vampire.

Sunlight damage, gaseous form, bat shape and other vampire transformations are
handled separately.

Scribe selects the archetype with "archetype vampire". Individual mobile fields
can override the inherited defaults normally.

Existing world vampires are not changed automatically.

The lich archetype gives an intelligent undead caster a physical, bloodless
body:

    < lich~ common~

A lich can still see, speak and use equipment, even though its heart, brain
and guts are gone. It is not mindless and does not move through ghost phases.

It resists dark effects and is especially vulnerable to holy power. Poison,
sleep, life drain, charm, cold and electricity do not affect it. Ordinary
nonmagical weapons cannot hurt it, while magical weapons use the normal
combat rules. This archetype does not add hold or paralysis immunity.

The template supplies spec_lich. When its combat special runs, it casts at the
opponent it is already fighting. It can use curse from the start, chill touch
at level 6, blindness at level 9, fireball at level 15 and acid blast at level
25. It will not try to curse someone already cursed, or blind someone already
blind or without eyes.

A lich cannot use this special while held, dazed, unable to act, non-corporeal
or in a cone_of_silence room. If it cannot cast, ordinary combat continues.

spec_lich adds +15 percent to the special-function XP calculation. The
archetype itself adds no extra XP. Existing mobiles keep any special you
explicitly gave them, and named liches elsewhere in the world are not changed
automatically.

A killed lich leaves a corpse marked as undead. Phylacteries and returning
after death are separate work.

The dragon body species gives a mobile four legs, wings, a tail and scales,
with ordinary claw, fang and tail attacks. Its forelegs work as legs rather
than humanoid arms. It does not choose a dragon's size, colour, breath weapon
or elemental defences.

The dracolich archetype uses that dragon body:

    < dracolich~ common~

It is intelligent and undead, and it keeps the dragon's wings, tail, scales
and natural attacks. It can see and speak, although its eyes are now points
of light in empty sockets. It has no living heart, brain or guts, and it does
not move through ghost phases.

Its defaults are:

    resistance:     RES_DARK
    vulnerability:  RES_HOLY
    immunity:       RES_POISON | RES_SLEEP | RES_DRAIN | RES_CHARM
                    | RES_COLD | RES_ELECTRICITY | RES_HOLD
                    | RES_PARALYSIS | RES_NONMAGIC

Ordinary nonmagical weapons cannot hurt it. Magical weapons use the normal
combat rules. Set any resistance or immunity inherited from its former
dragon type on the individual mobile; the archetype does not guess its
colour or breath.

The archetype supplies spec_dracolich. In combat it usually casts from the
lich spell list. On 20 percent of the turns when that special is selected, it
tries a paralysing gaze instead. The opponent needs working eyes and must not
already be held. An invisible opponent can still be affected. The gaze uses a
paralysis save, does no damage and works in a cone_of_silence room; spells do
not. If the gaze cannot be used, the dracolich tries a spell.

The special has a +15 percent XP value when it is the only special. If you add
a breath special, the usual weighted special-XP calculation applies. The
archetype has no separate XP adjustment.

When spec_dracolich is enabled, an unarmed claw, bite or tail hit that actually
hurts also carries a chill. The hit gains 2d8 cold damage, adjusted for the
target's cold defences. It can also paralyse the target for 2d6 combat rounds
on a failed paralysis save. Cold immunity stops the extra damage but not the
paralysis. Paralysis immunity stops the paralysis but not the cold. A target
already held is not paralysed again.

These contact effects do not take a special turn. Adding a breath special in
slot 2 does not halve their chance: they work on every qualifying natural hit
while spec_dracolich remains enabled. A miss, blocked or zero-damage hit, or
an attack with a weapon has no contact effect.

Choose the breath of the original dragon on each individual mobile. For
example, a former fire-breathing dragon can use:

    archetype dracolich
    sp2 spec_breath_fire
    spchance auto

That keeps spec_dracolich in slot 1 and puts fire breath in slot 2, with half
the special opportunities going to each. Other dragons should use their own
appropriate breath special. spec_breath_any chooses randomly, so it is not a
replacement for a known original breath. Set any original elemental defences
on the individual mobile as well. A standalone sp replaces the whole inherited
special set, so do not use it unless you mean to remove spec_dracolich.

The archetype also supplies ACT_FEAR_AURA. This is a general mobile trait,
not part of spec_dracolich. An opponent fighting the dracolich who can see it
makes one magic/psychic fear save per fight. Failure makes the opponent try
to flee. A builder can give the same aura to any other mobile with
act fear_aura. On an individual dracolich, act fear_aura instead cancels the
inherited flag. Replacing spec_dracolich does not remove the aura.

A successful gaze save does not yet make that target permanently immune to
this dracolich's gaze. Breath keeps DD4's existing dragon-breath timing; this
chunk adds no new cooldown. Undead control, a spirit host and return after
death are separate work.

A killed dracolich leaves a corpse marked as undead. Existing world
dracoliches, including Wyrenthoth, and existing living dragons are not changed
automatically.

The ghost archetype is currently a development-stage physiology template:

    < ghost~ common~

It inherits the humanoid body species and adds ACT_UNDEAD. Its body-form
XOR mask adds BODY_NO_HEART, BODY_NO_CORPSE and BODY_INORGANIC, while
cancelling the inherited PART_HEART, PART_BRAINS and PART_GUTS.

The remaining humanoid limb and sensory capabilities are retained.
Removing the physical brain part does not imply mindlessness. The
template adds neither AFF_MINDLESS nor BODY_NO_SPEECH.

Both heart changes are necessary: BODY_NO_HEART alone does not negate
an inherited PART_HEART under the current HAS_HEART() check.

The default ghost leaves no corpse or death-generated body parts.
Equipment and coins use the existing no-corpse death handling.
Do not introduce a separate ghost death or extraction path.

No additional AFF, attack-part, resistance or vulnerability defaults
are supplied. The ghost now inherits RES_NONMAGIC immunity, with
per-contact exceptions in the supported phase-aware weapon/shield paths.
It has no compulsory special functions or additional combat/XP modifiers.

Inherited initial phases, live phase storage and supported weapon/shield
contact rules are connected. Spell-phase handling, automatic
materialisation and active ghost abilities remain unfinished.
AFF_NON_CORPOREAL is not assigned or changed.

Do not convert existing world ghosts to the unfinished phased template
yet. Animate Dead uses dedicated ghost servant 87 with MobGhostPhase none,
which opts out of phase contacts and uses ordinary resistance rules.

Scribe selects it with "archetype ghost". Individual flag fields remain
XOR overrides. Repeating an inherited flag cancels it. Cancelling
ACT_UNDEAD does not automatically change the separate body properties.

Animate Dead selects skeleton 85 below level 35, ghoul 86 at levels 35-44,
and ghost 87 at level 45 and above. The ghost prototype selects the ghost
archetype and sets MobGhostPhase none. All three servants have no-XP and
no-quest flags; the spell supplies their existing HP/level scaling and
master/charm control. The ghost keeps its ordinary nonmagical immunity,
inorganic body and no-corpse handling without opting into phase contacts.
The old guardian vampire 3404 and existing world mobs are unchanged.

See the Scribe manual's "Ghost archetype: permanent physiology" section.


Ghost phase: initial defaults and live state
------------------------------------------

Initial phase resolves through body species, creature archetype,
individual mobile definition and prototype, then is copied into
each newly created NPC.

Phase is a scalar selection, not a bitmask. A later explicit value
replaces the earlier value; do not OR or XOR phases together.

The C-table initial_ghost_phase fields accept MOB_TEMPLATE_UNSET
for inheritance or one of GHOST_PHASE_NONE, GHOST_PHASE_ETHEREAL
and GHOST_PHASE_SEMI_MATERIAL. Both raw tables are validated at boot.

All current body species supply none. The ghost archetype supplies
semi_material for development testing; other archetypes inherit.
No name, vnum, undead flag or special function independently selects
a phase in create_mobile().

The optional Scribe mobile field is:

    ghostphase inherit
    ghostphase none
    ghostphase ethereal
    ghostphase semi_material

Use the appropriate single setting. Omission or inherit emits no
override. Explicit none is preserved.

The corresponding optional #MOBILES tail field is:

    MobGhostPhase <inherit|none|ethereal|semi_material>

This field has no trailing tilde. Place it after any mob programs,
archetype/rank selection and teacher entries, with the other optional
metadata before the next # record. Invalid phase tokens stop loading
with a diagnostic identifying the mobile.

MOB_INDEX_DATA preserves the original setting in area_ghost_phase
and the resolved initial value in initial_ghost_phase.
CHAR_DATA.ghost_phase remains the independent live value.

clear_char() still clears the live value to none. create_mobile()
then copies the validated prototype initial value once. PCs keep
the neutral value. Recreating an NPC restores its prototype's initial
phase; a normal reset does not overwrite an existing living instance.

Authorised live controls remain:

    mset <mobile> ghostphase none
    mset <mobile> ghostphase ethereal
    mset <mobile> ghostphase semi_material

Live mset does not accept inherit and does not edit the prototype.
mstat separates body-species, archetype, template, individual,
prototype-initial and current-live values.

Phase remains independent of ACT_UNDEAD, anatomy and resistance masks.
Explicit none does not remove undead classification or restore organs.
Cancelling ACT_UNDEAD does not automatically clear the selected phase.

The live phase now affects the explicitly supported weapon/shield
contacts and their pre-success contact checks.

A fully ethereal NPC and an unphased character cannot reach one another
through those physical contacts, even with a magical weapon.
Semi-material and ethereal NPCs share an ethereal side for this contact
policy; other matching immunities still apply.

Against a default semi-material ghost from the unphased side, mundane
weapons are blocked by RES_NONMAGIC. Mundane silver is admitted at half
damage. Magical weapons, including magical silver, receive normal
treatment unless another configured response applies.

A material vulnerability can also overcome RES_NONMAGIC. For example, a
cold-iron-vulnerable creature can be struck by a mundane cold-iron weapon.
Cold iron does not otherwise bypass the semi-material barrier.

These exceptions do not modify stored masks or make silver magical.
Any other matching immunity still wins. The conditional silver
resistance is combined once with other responses; a matching
vulnerability cancels it under the existing non-stacking rule.

Cancelling RES_NONMAGIC through an individual XOR override removes that
barrier and its conditional silver penalty. Selecting ghostphase none
alone does not remove the separately inherited immunity.

Stun rejects inorganic NPCs. Decapitate rejects NPCs that are both
inorganic and no-corpse, avoiding fabricated ghost heads. Player
profiles are unchanged. The material-only finishing/status chance
rules otherwise retain their existing scope after the contact gate.

Generic damage callers, spell-phase rules, unconverted projectiles,
Target/trauma and other bespoke effects are not automatically covered.
Existing mask-based effects still use their declared categories.
The phased ghost is not yet ready for conversion of production world mobs.
Animate Dead's dedicated ghost servant uses MobGhostPhase none instead.

There is no automatic transition, timer, new special or player phasing.
Visibility, movement, normal death and reward handling remain separate.

See the Scribe manual's "Ghost phase: weapon and shield contact" section
for the exact supported paths, contact matrix and tests.

See the Scribe manual's "Ghost phase: initial defaults and live state"
section.

Options for "rank" values (in increasing order of power) are "common", "rare",
"elite", "boss" and "world".

The 'hitroll', 'armor', 'hitnodice', 'hitsizeddice', 'hitplus', 'damnnodice',
'damsizedice', 'damroll', 'exp', 'position', and 'position' fields are
present for compatibility with original Diku mud, but their values are ignored
by the server--DD4 generates these values internally based on the level of the
mobile.

'sex':
    NEUTRAL           0
    MALE              1
    FEMALE            2

An example mob, demonstrating many of the features outlined above:

#27318
large black dragon Arlauthra~
Arlauthra~
Arlauthra the black dragon regards you with simmering fury.
~
Arlauthra the black dragon has been a bane upon the domain for decades now,
and now you stand before him, ready to mete out justice at long last.  He
appears abnormally slender in comparison to other chromatic dragons--wiry,
but not gaunt. His wicked horns protrude from the sides of his head and wrap
around, projecting forward. A large frill adorns the upper part of its neck.
He smells like nothing so much as rotting vegetation and foul water. He must
be 5 metres tall and 25 metres long. He is not at all pleased to see you here.
~
